##FILE-BEGIN## trading-research/pipeline/research/LOCAL_DEPLOY.md (6193 bytes)
# LOCAL_DEPLOY.md - run the L2 gate and C1-L2 pipeline on your machine

Why local: the Vibe sandbox resets between turns and wiped the raw L2 files
twice, burning the Tardis trial transfer quota (~20 GB) on re-downloads.
Your machine has no reset problem, real pandas/pyarrow, and one download
suffices. This guide reproduces exactly what the sandbox verified
(2026-10-01, 2026-10-10) and then extends to the full 10-day window.

## 0. One-time setup

    git clone https://github.com/andrea-karlvassallo20-beep/vp-mr-strategy
    cd vp-mr-strategy
    python3 -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate
    pip install pandas pyarrow scipy numpy lifelines

Copy the sandbox-verified scripts from the Vibe knowledge tree
(pipeline/research/l2gate/) into the repo next to src/:
checkpoints.awk, gate.awk, run_day.sh, c1l2_dryrun.py, convert_to_parquet.py.

## 1. Data acquisition (Tardis)

You need a paid Tardis subscription or a fresh key (the trial key from chat
is quota-dead and must be rotated regardless). Put the key in an env var,
never in a file:

    export TARDIS_KEY="TD...."

Free files: the 1st of each month needs no key at all. 2026-10-01 is free;
2026-10-02..10 need the key. Per day (binance-futures BTCUSDT):

    curl -sS --max-time 120 -H "Authorization: Bearer $TARDIS_KEY" \
      -o incremental_book_L2_2026-10-DD.csv.gz \
      "https://datasets.tardis.dev/v1/binance-futures/incremental_book_L2/2026/10/DD/BTCUSDT.csv.gz"
    curl -sS --max-time 90 -H "Authorization: Bearer $TARDIS_KEY" \
      -o book_snapshot_25_2026-10-DD.csv.gz \
      "https://datasets.tardis.dev/v1/binance-futures/book_snapshot_25/2026/10/DD/BTCUSDT.csv.gz"
    curl -sS --max-time 60 -H "Authorization: Bearer $TARDIS_KEY" \
      -o trades_2026-10-DD.csv.gz \
      "https://datasets.tardis.dev/v1/binance-futures/trades/2026/10/DD/BTCUSDT.csv.gz"

Do NOT use -L together with the Authorization header (the S3 redirect
rejects that combination). Byte sizes the sandbox verified (2026-10-01):
trades 24,112,509; book_snapshot_25 90,294,106; incremental_book_L2
796,135,847. Day totals run ~0.5-1 GB per day for the L2 diffs; the full
10-day set is ~8 GB.

## 2. The gate (run this FIRST, nothing downstream before it passes)

CRITICAL schema warning, verified against the repo source: your repo's
src/verify_l2_gate.py auto-detects two schemas - Binance-native diffs with
seq_u/seq_pu, or Tardis-style sequence_number/operation columns. The Tardis
datasets incremental_book_L2 CSV is NEITHER: its columns are
exchange,symbol,timestamp,local_timestamp,is_snapshot,side,price,amount
with no sequence numbers. verify_l2_gate.py as written will refuse it
("unknown diff schema"). Two correct options:

Option A (repo-native): run src/gate_tardis_sample.py (built exactly for
these CSVs; validates criteria 2 and 3 against book_snapshot_25; criterion
1 is structurally N/A on this feed - the CSVs carry no U/u/pu).

    python src/gate_tardis_sample.py \
      --diffs data/raw/incremental_book_L2_2026-10-01.csv.gz \
      --snapshots data/raw/book_snapshot_25_2026-10-01.csv.gz

Option B (sandbox-verified, deterministic, seed 424242): the mawk streaming
gate. Bounded memory, no pandas needed, byte-reproducible:

    mkdir -p gate_out
    sh run_day.sh 2026-10-01   # downloads + checkpoints + gate, writes gate_out/

Edit run_day.sh first if the raw files are already downloaded (skip the
curl lines). Parity anchors the sandbox established (your run MUST
reproduce these): 2026-10-01: rows_total 145,203,455, mid 100/100, depth
100/100, 5000/5000 levels exact, snapshot_blocks 4 (initial + 3 mid-day
resyncs with pre-resync gaps 0.476s / 0.512s / 2.497s), EXIT 0.
2026-10-10: rows_total 78,904,284, 1 block, max update gap 0.052s, mid
100/100, depth 100/100, EXIT 0.

Gate semantics: 100 seeded checkpoints per day, mid within one tick (0.1),
per-level size within 5 percent rel tol for levels within 10bps of mid,
pass at >= 99/100 per criterion; is_snapshot=true rows are resync blocks
that clear the book; apply all updates with ts <= checkpoint ts before
comparing (checkpoints collide with 11-480 same-microsecond updates).

## 3. Parquet conversion (optional, for speed)

    python convert_to_parquet.py data/raw data/processed

The stored script is authored-verified against the real schema
(chunksize 1M, zstd, int64 microsecond timestamps) but was never executed
in the sandbox (no pyarrow there, pip forbidden). First execution is on
your machine - treat the first run as its validation.

## 4. C1-L2 pipeline

The registered harness is src/c1_l2_test.py + src/cox_diagnostics.py
(lifelines for the stratified fit). The spec (paper/research/
c1_l2_test_spec.md) needs 2-4 weeks of continuous gated data: the H=24h
label horizon with H-length purge and embargo empties every training fold
on shorter windows (the sandbox dry run proved exactly this on one day),
and the compression percentile pool needs 5 trailing days per
hour-of-day bucket. With the full 10 days you are still at the short end;
expect the pool to be thin and label that in any output. Kill lines raise
AssertionError BY DESIGN when they fire - a raised kill line is the harness
working, not crashing.

For a machinery smoke test before the full download, the stdlib dry-run
harness (c1_l2_dryrun.py, no dependencies at all) reproduces the sandbox
run on the free 2026-10-01 files: 74 decision rows, 38 events at H=24h,
47 events at H=2h, kill lines 1-5 all executing (2, 4, 5 fired on the
one-day sample; 3 correctly reported not evaluable, no powered folds).
Its numbers are explicitly NOT research results.

## 5. Order of operations

1. Download one day. 2. Gate it (Option A or B). 3. If exit 0, proceed;
if not, stop and log - never patch data. 4. Repeat per day. 5. Convert to
parquet if desired. 6. Run the C1-L2 harness on the full window with a
dated amendment per the frozen protocol. 7. Commit gaps.log, gate tables
and exit codes to the repo so the record outlives any environment.

## 6. Macro censor

2026-10-02 contains NFP at 12:30 UTC. When your window includes event
days, apply src/macro_censor.py with data/macro_timeline.csv (16 events,
surprise_note dropped at ingestion, assert-empty).

## Stage 2, event study (complete; holdout sealed)

H1 NOT SUPPORTED: no state carries the 0.15-sigma weekly edge in either
window (momentum_up IS +0.056 sigma vs +0.052 unconditional; VAL +0.049 vs
+0.111). H2 NOT SUPPORTED: within-state autocorrelation ~0 or negative.
Structure: IS momentum_down +0.229 sigma next-week (boot p = 0.029),

(mean run 45 weeks). Post-2014 the machine collapses to two states
(momentum_down/reversal_up zero occupancy 2014-2020). sp_z valid from 2015
at default normWin; no z gradient in either window.

## Stage 3, base-case backtest (complete)

Engine hand-verified against bar data (2017-02-24 entry, 2018-10-26 exit,
2009/2010 short entries reconstruct exactly).

A0 (state system, no z filter): IS 2007-2013: 6 trades, Sharpe -0.115,
total -7.6%, DD -33% (buy-hold 0.406, momentum 0.788, random-entry
matched-duration median 0.23). VAL 2014-2020: 6 long trades, Sharpe 0.420,
total +31% (buy-hold 1.027, momentum 0.505, random median 0.465). A0 is
worse than random in IS and indistinguishable from random in VAL.

A1 (pre-registered |sp_z|>=1 transition filter): zero trades ever.
Structural reason: transitions occur at spread ~ 0 hence sp_z ~ 0. The
pre-registered rule set A is vacuous as written. A1t (membership entries
with the z filter): 3 trades, Sharpe 0.107, below the random median 0.502.

Verdict: rule set A killed as an edge claim, consistent with stage 2.

## Stage 4a, sensitivity (complete)

H4: v3 and v2 machines agree only 57% of the time; v2 shows the same shape
(IS bear bounce stronger, +0.40 sigma over n=33; VAL both states sit at the
unconditional drift). v3 does not dominate v2 on weekly information;
neither carries a stable weekly edge. v3 stays preferable on invariance
grounds only.

H5: 27-combo grid, A0 Sharpe 2007-2020: best 0.629 (hlVol 15, hlFlow 130,
emaLen 210; 6 trades), default 0.153 (rank 11/27), plateau fraction 0.11.
Mean Sharpe by emaLen 52/105/210 = 0.00/0.05/0.40: pure slowness artifact
tracking the drift. No plateau, H5 fails.

H6: momentum_down exists almost only in high-vol regimes (86/90 weeks,
+0.25 sigma; low-vol n=4). Edge survives excluding literal crash weeks
(+0.223, n=79) because the whole state belongs to the one bear era.
reversal_down splits +0.138 lo-vol vs +0.009 hi-vol, both near drift.
All conditional structure is regime-concentrated.

normWin=150 variant: z valid from 2008-07-25. Buckets 2008-2020 flat
(z<=-2: +0.09; |z|<1: +0.10; z>=2: -0.03). No weekly z-gradient even with
7 extra years. Objection closed.

## Stage 5, cost and execution stress (complete)

Slippage 1 -> 3 ticks per side on A0, A1t, and C2: Sharpe moves at most
0.012 (C2 IS 0.788 -> 0.776). Costs are immaterial at the weekly horizon;
per-side costs of 0.36-0.86 pts on a 2000-12000 pt instrument with
multi-week holds are noise. Signal quality is the binding constraint, not
execution. Close-fill vs next-open differences immaterial throughout.

## Stage 6a, trial registry and statistical reliability (complete; holdout sealed)

Registry: 8 pre-registered stage-3 rule evaluations + 27 searched grid
combinations = 35 rule evaluations; controls (C1/C2/C3) are benchmarks,
not candidate signals; the holdout has never been touched.

Individual demeaned stationary block bootstrap p-values (Sharpe > 0,
one-sided, B=2000, block 13, seed 42), with PSR (skew/kurt adjusted):
- A0 IS: Sharpe -0.115, p 0.61.
- A0 VAL: Sharpe 0.420, p 0.099, PSR 0.86. Not significant, and stage 3
  showed it equals the random-entry control.
- A1t VALz: Sharpe 0.108, p 0.41. Dead.
- C2 momentum IS: Sharpe 0.798, p 0.033, PSR 0.97.
- C2 momentum VAL: Sharpe 0.508, p 0.053, PSR 0.90.
- C1 buy-hold IS: 0.407, p 0.21 (the 2007-2013 lost decade); VAL: 1.029,
  p 0.0005.

White-style reality check on the 27-combo searched family (positions fixed,
demeaned returns resampled, B=1000): observed family best Sharpe 0.58
(common-window aligned), null max p95 = 0.67, RC p = 0.11. The searched
family contains nothing that survives multiplicity adjustment.

Net reading: every AVFI-derived variant fails individually and the family
fails jointly. The momentum control is the only variant significant in
both windows, consistent with the decades-old trend-following prior, not
with anything the indicator adds.

## Stage 4b: rule set T robustness (unsealed data only, complete)

Registered rule set T on 2007-2020 (IS+VAL, next-open fills, costs in):
- Sharpe 0.571, total +154%, max drawdown -23.5%, exposure 72%, 39 trades.
- Close-fill vs next-open: 0.556 vs 0.571. Immaterial, consistent with
  stage 5.
- Leave-one-year-out Sharpe ranges 0.445-0.686; excluding 2008-2009
  entirely still gives 0.50. The effect is NOT one-episode: it survives
  every single-year exclusion, unlike the AVFI capitulation structure.
- Yearly profile: beats buy-and-hold in 3 of 14 years (2008, 2011, 2018),
  underperforms in 11. T is a defensive variant of buy-and-hold: it earns
  in the bad years, bleeds modestly in good ones. Investment case is
  drawdown control (max DD -23.5% vs buy-hold -52% in IS) plus the
  trend-following literature prior, not return maximisation.
- Vol interaction: returns concentrate in low-vol in-position weeks
  (+0.37%/week vs +0.06% in high-vol); flat high-vol weeks gained +0.54%/wk
  (sitting out volatile weeks avoided some large up-weeks, e.g. 2009/2020
  recoveries). Logged as a descriptive caveat for the battery
  interpretation.
- Costs: 55.8 pts over 13 years, 1.3% of average close. Immaterial.
- Longest flat spell: 21 weeks. Whipsaw is real but bounded.

## 2026-11-11 (block 19): Sunday-sequence rehearsal; two real booking-tool bugs found and fixed pre-first-run; fetch-staleness guards added
- Session context: Wednesday 2026-11-11 (the 2026-11-13 decision close has not happened; no Sunday-log entry or outcome due, none fabricated; Entry 0 stands). Concrete step: rehearse the FIRST regular Sunday session's three-tool sequence (stage0c -> stage7_outcome.py --append -> stage7_log_entry.py --append) end to end before it runs for real, since each tool was validated individually but the sequence handoffs never were.
- BUG 1 FOUND AND FIXED (stage7_log_entry.py, mechanical date derivation; rule/signal/sizing math untouched): next_friday/next_monday were derived from TODAY's weekday, not from the decision close. On a Sunday run the entry would have logged its planned fill at "Monday decision-close+10" (2026-11-23 for dc 2026-11-13) while the outcome resolver correctly books the week labeled 2026-11-20 (Monday open 2026-11-16) - the log text would contradict the booked fill, and the planned action would have covered the WRONG week, silently skipping a week of the 26-entry forward test. Fix: fill Monday = decision close + 3 days; next decision close = decision close + 7 days (template semantics, run-date independent). Entry 0 (hand-written at start-up) already followed this convention and is untouched.
- BUG 2 FOUND AND FIXED (stage7_outcome.py, pending-selection logic): the oldest-pending search matched any block containing "- Outcome: pending", but that line stays in a resolved entry forever (the log is append-only and never edited). After the first resolution the resolver would have re-found resolved Entry 0 every week, hit its own "already resolved" refusal, and never resolved Entry 1 or any later week - the forward test would have stalled after one resolution. Fix: an entry is pending iff it has the pending line AND no resolved-outcome block.
- NEW GUARD (fetch staleness, stage7_log_entry.py + stage7_outcome.py refuse, stage7_signal_snapshot.py warns): if the canonical data's fetch (stage0c_report.json fetched_at_utc) predates the decision/resolving week's Friday close, the booking tools REFUSE with a rerun-stage0c instruction. This closes the hole where a failed Sunday stage0c refresh would have booked the current PARTIAL 2026-11-13 bar (n_sessions=3, fetched Wed 2026-11-11) as a completed week. Deliberately keyed on the fetch DATE, not n_sessions (holiday weeks legitimately have 4 sessions).
- VALIDATION (no Python; same two-layer pattern as sessions 2/3/6/9): research/stage7_sunday_rehearsal_ts_mirror.ts executed on real bundle fixtures (log lines, canonical CSV tail, stage0c fetched_at; the 2026-11-13 bar values SYNTHETIC and labelled so, nothing booked): 25/25 checks pass (report research/stage7_sunday_rehearsal_report.json). ## 2026-11-11 (block 20): pre-signing power readouts for Amendment 4 (H10a) and Amendment 5 (H11a); H10a test found INVALID as drafted; H11a power quantified at ~1.25 sigma MDE

- Both unsigned amendments pre-commit "power readout FIRST, shown to Andrea before any outcome". research/power_readout_h10_h11.py + research/power_readout_h10_h11_report.json + docs/amendment4_5_power_readout.md deliver it. Unsealed data only; no H10a/H11a outcome computed; the G2Top events' forward returns were never read (synthetic events are control-pool draws plus assumed shifts, MC seed 20261111); holdout untouched; the registered H11a null was reproduced exactly (seed 42, B 2000, block 13, cond_mean_p draw order).
- H11a (G2Top n=11 vs 68 fresh-high control weeks, stage2b v2 test): correctly sized (FP 5.8% at zero effect). Power: 7% at 0.10 sigma, 13% at 0.30, 22% at 0.50, 63% at 1.00, 96% at 1.50; MDE at 80% power ~1.25 sigma (~4% absolute 4-week return gap). Economically meaningful effects (0.1-0.3 sigma) are detected 7-13% of the time; a null result would be uninformative. Confirms and quantifies the amendment's own honest prior. Recommendation to Andrea: withdraw or sign knowing it only detects implausible effects; no re-tuning either way.
- H10a (sp_z_clv vs sp_z against forward 8-week realized vol, n=299): the AS-DRAFTED test (paired stationary block bootstrap, block 13, demeaned, one-sided 5%) is MISCALIBRATED on the registered series structure: measured FP 16%/12%/7% at base rho 0.10/0.20/0.30 vs nominal 5%. Mechanism measured: sp_z lag-1 ~0.985, effective sample size ~2 weeks over 299, predictors correlate 0.934; the true sampling variance of the Fisher-z difference is ~2.2x the within-realization bootstrap variance. Longer blocks do NOT fix it (FP 11-20% at mean blocks 13/67/130/260): within-one-realization resampling cannot reproduce across-realization variance for near-integrated series. Recommendation: do not sign Amendment 4 as drafted; a revised draft must use a persistence-preserving null.
- Persistence-preserving circular-shift (Theiler-style) alignment null validated in the same readout: correctly sized (FP 5.3-6.7%) with power 21-29% at true rho-difference 0.10, 60-68% at 0.20, 97-100% at 0.30; weak below 0.05. This is the concrete revision path if Andrea wants H10a at all; 80% power needs a true rho difference around 0.15-0.20.
- Programme-level lesson (generalizes): any correlation-style test on the smooth weekly registered series at n~300 inherits the same invalidity; future hypotheses must use persistence-preserving nulls or event-study machinery, never within-sample i.i.d.-flavored resampling.
- INTEGRITY NOTE: the readout script's first run had a surrogate bug (second predictor's AR(1) innovations missing the innovation scale, building a systematic correlation difference into the null runs, inflating the H10a FP numbers to 27-68%). Caught by the target-vs-realized validity checks added for exactly that purpose, fixed before any number was shown or synced; all published numbers are from corrected runs. H11a numbers were unaffected (no surrogate).
- Not added to verify_all.sh: Monte Carlo readouts are stochastic and slow; the deterministic chain is unaffected. Discipline: no rule/default/hypothesis changed; paper trading only; Sunday cadence 2026-11-15 unchanged (stage0c refresh -> stage7_outcome --append -> stage7_log_entry --append).

## 2026-11-12 (block 35): The inevitability question: Andrea's "inevitable-occurrence / impossible-drawdown" framework examined and answered from the record; no research work, nothing booked

- Andrea directive: whether, after the nulls, a strategy can be built from "inevitable occurrences" or conditions "impossible to result in drawdown": impulse-correction alternation, crash as the best long, the last orderbook low before a never-remet price break, a 5-minute time stop, compression inevitably needing expansion - and his standing belief that AVFI confers information about tops and bottoms.
- Answer delivered from the record (no new computation): the core category error is that inevitability without timing and direction is not edge, and inevitability with timing and direction does not exist in real time (hindsight identity). Each item mapped: impulse/correction and compression-expansion are true but directionless and already implemented as state-dependent sizing (V throttle, V4h graduated target, Lesson 10); crash-as-best-long is the H7 lesson (one episode, null at the battery, US-index survivorship caveat, "unexpectedly" contradicts conditioning); the last-never-remet low is a hindsight identity whose real-time version is a confirmed breakout, i.e. exactly rule set T, the only replicated effect, which failed the holdout; the 5-minute time stop is a legitimate risk tool (bounds time exposure, not loss) and is untestable in this programme (no intraday/orderbook data, no backtest without a signed amendment, automation caps at once/day, costs dominate at that horizon); "impossible to result in drawdown" has no non-trivial instance (only no position; Patterson's canon is the case file for funds that believed otherwise).
- AVFI tops/bottoms belief vs evidence, stated plainly from the executed artifacts: H8 flow-fade (divTop n=32): 4-week forward returns NOT below the control (event +0.17 sigma ABOVE, p=0.79; family-adjusted 0.89); H9 climax absorption: n=3 top events in 26 years (p=0.082 unadjusted, 0.089 family) - an anecdote quota, not evidence; bottom arm same verdict; H8/H9 frozen nulls, never to be re-run. The belief is recorded as a belief; the evidence is recorded as evidence; the programme keeps them separate. AVFI's residual role: regime descriptor.
- Programme consequences: none. No hypothesis registered from the inevitability framework (nothing in it is testable that has not already been tested or is not forward-only design); no track changes; family cap stays at three (T, V, V4h); the FTEP posture stands (few registrations, long tracks). - THE CORE ALGEBRA, derived and numerically verified on synthetic random bars (no market data): with the prior close inside the bar, rawDelta = volume * ((high + low) - 2*prevClose) / (high - low) (max abs error 6.4e-9 over 200k bars); THE CLOSE NEVER ENTERS THE FORMULA - the "delta" is volume-weighted candle anatomy (where the bar midpoint sits relative to the prior close), not order flow; gap bars are always signed in the gap direction, muted by gap extent (gap-up: +volume*(high-low)/(high-prevClose), error 9.1e-13); |rawDelta| <= volume. Consequences recorded: reversal bars are misread by construction (an upthrust closing on its lows prints as buying) - the exact failure mode that matters for a turning-point programme; overnight gaps mechanically pump the cumulative in the gap direction; the AVFI honesty class applies (derived geometry, no aggressive-flow classification).
